\part{Single-Answer Questions}
\section{Network Security Concepts}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
In a broad sense, network security refers to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Physical security of devices only
\item Cybersecurity / cyberspace security at a national level
\item Only firewall deployment
\item Only antivirus protection
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Broadly, network security is synonymous with cybersecurity, covering national laws, regulations, and processes to protect cyberspace.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
In a narrow sense, network security focuses on:

\begin{qoptions}
\item National cyberspace policies only
\item Vendor devices and solutions that ensure secure running of enterprise networks
\item Only social engineering defense
\item Only data-center cooling
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} In a narrow sense, network security refers to measures such as firewalls, IPS, VPNs, and policies that protect enterprise networks.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which period of security history mainly used stream ciphers and physical protection?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Information security period
\item Communication security period
\item Cyberspace security period
\item Information assurance period
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The communication security period (1940s) relied on physical security and cryptographic communication, mainly stream ciphers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
During the information security period, which three goals were the main focus?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Confidentiality, integrity, availability
\item Speed, bandwidth, latency
\item Power, cooling, cabling
\item Routing, switching, NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The classic CIA triad — confidentiality, integrity, and availability — became the core objectives of information security.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which two properties were added to the CIA triad during the information assurance period?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Controllability and non-repudiation
\item Speed and redundancy
\item Encryption and hashing
\item NAT and VLAN
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} In the information assurance period, controllability (monitoring and control) and non-repudiation (proof of actions) joined the CIA triad.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
The cyberspace security period aims to protect:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Only web servers
\item Only user passwords
\item Facilities, data, users, and operations across cyberspace
\item Only email gateways
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Cyberspace security expands protection to the entire digital ecosystem: facilities, data, users, and operations.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which term describes the practice of protecting networks and data from unauthorized access?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cybersecurity
\item Marketing
\item Accounting
\item Logistics
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Cybersecurity protects networks, devices, and data from unauthorized access or attack.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which of the following best defines a vulnerability?

\begin{qoptions}
\item A weakness that can be exploited
\item A security patch
\item A firewall rule
\item A network diagram
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} A vulnerability is a weakness in a system that a threat actor can exploit.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What is an exploit?

\begin{qoptions}
\item A software patch
\item A method to take advantage of a vulnerability
\item A type of firewall
\item A routing protocol
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} An exploit is a technique or code that leverages a vulnerability.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which term describes a potential danger to an asset?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Risk
\item Threat
\item Vulnerability
\item Control
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A threat is any potential danger that could exploit a vulnerability.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which term describes the likelihood and impact of a threat exploiting a vulnerability?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Threat
\item Risk
\item Asset
\item Control
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Risk measures the probability and impact of a security incident.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Future security architecture is moving toward:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Single perimeter defense
\item Zero-trust and dynamic trust models
\item Open anonymous access
\item No logging
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Zero trust assumes no implicit trust and verifies every access request dynamically.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which technology is increasingly used to detect unknown threats?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Signature-only antivirus
\item Artificial intelligence and machine learning
\item Hub-based networks
\item Plaintext protocols
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} AI/ML helps analyze behavior and detect anomalies and unknown threats.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What does confidentiality ensure?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Only speed
\item Information is accessible only to authorized users
\item Unlimited access
\item Data duplication
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Confidentiality ensures that information is disclosed only to authorized individuals.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What does integrity ensure?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Fast transmission
\item Information is not tampered with during transmission/storage
\item Free access
\item Unencrypted storage
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Integrity ensures data is not altered or destroyed in an unauthorized manner.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What does availability ensure?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Authorized users can access information when needed
\item All users can access
\item Systems are offline
\item Data is hidden
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Availability guarantees reliable and timely access to information for authorized users.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What does controllability mean in information security?

\begin{qoptions}
\item No monitoring
\item Ability to monitor and control information/systems
\item Unlimited downloads
\item No access controls
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Controllability enables organizations to monitor, manage, and protect information and systems.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
What does non-repudiation prevent?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Fast data transfer
\item Senders or receivers from denying their actions
\item Encryption
\item Backups
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Non-repudiation provides proof of origin and delivery so parties cannot deny participation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
The broad definition of network security is closest to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cybersecurity
\item Firewall configuration
\item Antivirus deployment
\item Physical security
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Broadly, network security equates to cybersecurity covering national and societal cyberspace protection.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
The narrow definition of network security focuses on:

\begin{qoptions}
\item National laws only
\item Enterprise network protection solutions
\item Social media policies
\item Hardware warranties
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Narrowly, network security means vendor solutions and measures that keep enterprise networks secure.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
During which period did communication cipher security emerge?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cyberspace security period
\item Communication security period
\item Information assurance period
\item Cloud security period
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Stream ciphers and communication confidentiality appeared during the communication security period.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
The information security period added which goal to the earlier communication security focus?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Availability
\item Confidentiality, integrity, and availability
\item Non-repudiation only
\item Controllability only
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The information security period formalized confidentiality, integrity, and availability as core goals.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Controllability and non-repudiation became prominent during:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Communication security period
\item Information security period
\item Information assurance period
\item Cloud computing period
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} The information assurance period expanded security to include monitoring and proof of actions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is synonymous with national cyberspace security?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cyberspace security
\item Vulnerability
\item Broad network security
\item Integrity
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Broad network security refers to the concept where is synonymous with national cyberspace security.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: focuses on enterprise network protection solutions?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Narrow network security
\item Broad network security
\item Cyberspace security
\item Controllability
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Narrow network security refers to the concept where focuses on enterprise network protection solutions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: consists of confidentiality, integrity, and availability?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Controllability
\item Availability
\item Future security trend
\item CIA triad
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} CIA triad refers to the concept where consists of confidentiality, integrity, and availability.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: added controllability and non-repudiation?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Non-repudiation
\item Information assurance period
\item Future security trend
\item AI and machine learning
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Information assurance period refers to the concept where added controllability and non-repudiation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: protects facilities, data, users, and operations?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Future security trend
\item CIA triad
\item Narrow network security
\item Cyberspace security
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Cyberspace security refers to the concept where protects facilities, data, users, and operations.

Which concept is described as: ensures information is accessible only to authorized users?

\begin{qoptions}

\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Confidentiality refers to the concept where ensures information is accessible only to authorized users.

\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 30} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: ensures data is not tampered with during transmission or storage?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Narrow network security
\item Availability
\item Non-repudiation
\item Integrity
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Integrity refers to the concept where ensures data is not tampered with during transmission or storage.

\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 31} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Concepts}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: ensures authorized users can access information when needed?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Risk
\item Availability
\item Exploit

\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Availability refers to the concept where ensures authorized users can access information when needed.

\begin{qoptions}

\begin{explainbox}{B}

\section{Network Security Standards}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
ISO 27001 specifies requirements for:

\begin{qoptions}
\item An information security management system (ISMS)
\item A firewall hardware model
\item A routing protocol
\item A programming language
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO/IEC 27001 is the international standard for establishing, implementing, maintaining, and continually improving an ISMS.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
ISO 27002 is best described as:

\begin{qoptions}
\item A certification standard
\item A code of practice with security controls
\item A firewall configuration guide
\item A wireless protocol
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO/IEC 27002 provides best-practice security controls, while ISO 27001 is the certifiable requirements standard.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Cybersecurity Classified Protection 2.0 is issued by:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Huawei
\item China (as a national standard/policy)
\item IEEE
\item IETF
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection 2.0 is a Chinese national cybersecurity baseline and compliance framework.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Classified Protection 2.0 uses a protection-level scale from:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Level 1 to Level 5
\item Level 0 to Level 4
\item Level A to Level E
\item Level I to Level V
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The Chinese classified protection system defines five levels, with Level 5 being the highest.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which of the following is NOT a typical Classified Protection 2.0 lifecycle phase?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Classification
\item Filing
\item Construction and rectification
\item Marketing and sales
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} The Classified Protection lifecycle includes classification, filing, construction/rectification, level assessment, and supervision/inspection.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which ISO standard family addresses information security management?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ISO 9000
\item ISO 27000
\item ISO 14000
\item ISO 20000
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The ISO/IEC 27000 family covers information security management systems and controls.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which Chinese regulation is the basis for Classified Protection?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item GDPR
\item HIPAA
\item PCI DSS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} China Cybersecurity Law underpins the Classified Protection compliance framework.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
ISO 27001 uses which methodology for risk management?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Ignore risks
\item Plan-Do-Check-Act (PDCA)
\item Random patching
\item No reviews
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27001 follows the PDCA cycle for continuous improvement of the ISMS.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which document in ISO 27000 family provides implementation guidance for controls?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ISO 27001
\item ISO 27002
\item ISO 27003
\item ISO 27005
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27002 lists security controls and implementation guidance.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which is a key principle of Classified Protection 2.0?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Security synchronization with construction
\item Security as an afterthought
\item No auditing
\item Anonymous access
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection requires security to be planned, constructed, and used simultaneously with information systems.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
In Classified Protection 2.0, the security protection level is preliminarily determined by the network operator, but when the level reaches which level or above, expert review and approval are required?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Level 1
\item Level 2
\item Level 3
\item Level 4
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} For protection Level 2 and above, the preliminary determination must be reviewed and approved; Level 1 can be determined by the operator.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which standard is used to certify an ISMS?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ISO 27001
\item ISO 27002
\item ISO 9001
\item ISO 14001
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27001 contains the certifiable requirements for an ISMS.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
ISO 27002 is primarily a:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Certification standard
\item Code of practice for security controls
\item Risk assessment methodology
\item Networking protocol
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27002 provides best-practice controls but is not itself a certification standard.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Classified Protection 2.0 compliance is primarily relevant in:

\begin{qoptions}
\item China
\item United States only
\item European Union only
\item Every country equally
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection 2.0 is a Chinese national cybersecurity compliance framework.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which is the highest protection level in Classified Protection 2.0?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Level 1
\item Level 3
\item Level 5
\item Level 10
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection defines five levels, with Level 5 being the most stringent.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Expert review is required for Classified Protection levels starting at:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Level 1
\item Level 2
\item Level 3
\item Level 4
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} For Level 2 and above, expert review and approval are required.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: specifies certifiable ISMS requirements?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item ISO 27001
\item Level 2 and above
\item ISO 27002
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27001 refers to the concept where specifies certifiable ISMS requirements.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides a code of practice for security controls?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Classified Protection 2.0
\item ISO 27001:2022
\item ISO 27001
\item ISO 27002
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27002 refers to the concept where provides a code of practice for security controls.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is the continuous improvement methodology used in ISO 27001?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ISO 27001
\item Security synchronization
\item PDCA
\item ISO 27002
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} PDCA refers to the concept where is the continuous improvement methodology used in ISO 27001.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is a Chinese national cybersecurity framework?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Classified Protection 2.0
\item Level 2 and above
\item Classified Protection lifecycle
\item PDCA
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection 2.0 refers to the concept where is a Chinese national cybersecurity framework.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: range from Level 1 to Level 5?

\begin{qoptions}
\item PDCA
\item Classified Protection lifecycle
\item Classified Protection levels
\item Cybersecurity Law
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection levels refers to the concept where range from Level 1 to Level 5.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: require expert review and approval?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item PDCA
\item ISO 27001
\item Level 2 and above
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Level 2 and above refers to the concept where require expert review and approval.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: includes classification, filing, construction, assessment, and supervision?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Classified Protection lifecycle
\item ISO 27001
\item Security synchronization
\item ISO 27001:2022
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Classified Protection lifecycle refers to the concept where includes classification, filing, construction, assessment, and supervision.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: means planning security with information system construction?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Classified Protection levels
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item Security synchronization
\item Classified Protection 2.0
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Security synchronization refers to the concept where means planning security with information system construction.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: organizes controls into four themes?

\begin{qoptions}
\item PDCA
\item ISO 27001:2022
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item ISO 27002
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ISO 27001:2022 refers to the concept where organizes controls into four themes.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Security Standards}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: underpins Classified Protection in China?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Security synchronization
\item Cybersecurity Law
\item Classified Protection levels
\item Level 2 and above

\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Cybersecurity Law refers to the concept where underpins Classified Protection in China.
\end{explainbox}

\section{Network Fundamentals}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
What is the default subnet mask for a Class C IPv4 address?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 255.0.0.0
\item 255.255.0.0
\item 255.255.255.0
\item 255.255.255.255
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Class C networks use a default /24 subnet mask: 255.255.255.0.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol is used to automatically assign IPv6 addresses using MAC addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DHCPv6
\item SLAAC
\item ARP
\item DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} SLAAC can generate IPv6 addresses from MAC addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which layer-2 protocol prevents loops in redundant Ethernet topologies?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VTP
\item STP
\item RSTP
\item Both STP and RSTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Spanning Tree Protocol and Rapid STP prevent Layer 2 loops.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
What does VLAN stand for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Virtual Local Area Network
\item Virtual Large Area Network
\item Verified Local Access Node
\item Virtual Link Aggregation Network
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VLANs logically segment a switch into separate broadcast domains.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which device interconnects VLANs at Layer 3?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Layer 2 switch
\item Router or Layer 3 switch
\item Hub
\item Bridge
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Routers or Layer 3 switches perform inter-VLAN routing.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which OSI layer is responsible for reliable data transfer between hosts?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Network
\item Transport
\item Data link
\item Physical
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The transport layer manages end-to-end reliability, flow control, and error recovery.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
In the TCP/IP model, which layer corresponds to the OSI network layer?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Internet layer
\item Network interface layer
\item Transport layer
\item Application layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The TCP/IP internet layer maps to the OSI network layer and handles IP routing.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which layer handles encryption and data format conversion?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Application
\item Presentation
\item Session
\item Transport
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The presentation layer handles syntax, encryption, compression, and character encoding.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which layer establishes, manages, and terminates application dialogs?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Application
\item Presentation
\item Session
\item Transport
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} The session layer controls dialog establishment, maintenance, and termination.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol is used to send email between servers?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HTTP
\item SMTP
\item POP3
\item IMAP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} SMTP is used to send and relay email.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol is used by clients to retrieve email from a server while keeping messages on the server?

\begin{qoptions}
\item POP3
\item IMAP
\item SMTP
\item SNMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} IMAP allows clients to access and manage email while messages remain on the server.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol dynamically assigns IP addresses to hosts?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DNS
\item DHCP
\item ARP
\item FTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} DHCP dynamically assigns IP addresses and network configuration parameters.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol maps IP addresses to MAC addresses on a local network?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DNS
\item ARP
\item DHCP
\item ICMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ARP resolves IP addresses to MAC addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which protocol is used for network management and monitoring?

\begin{qoptions}
\item SNMP
\item SMTP
\item FTP
\item Telnet
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} SNMP manages and monitors network devices.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
What does ICMP primarily provide?

\begin{qoptions}
\item File transfer
\item Error reporting and diagnostics
\item Email delivery
\item Web browsing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ICMP reports errors and provides diagnostic functions such as ping and traceroute.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which port does HTTP use by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 20
\item 21
\item 80
\item 443
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} HTTP uses TCP port 80 by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which port does HTTPS use by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 80
\item 443
\item 22
\item 25
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} HTTPS uses TCP port 443 by default, providing encrypted web traffic.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which port does SSH use by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 21
\item 22
\item 23
\item 25
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} SSH uses TCP port 22 for secure remote access.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Telnet uses which port and is considered insecure because it transmits data in plaintext?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 21
\item 22
\item 23
\item 25
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Telnet uses TCP port 23 and sends data, including credentials, in plaintext.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which address is a Layer 2 address?

\begin{qoptions}
\item IP address
\item MAC address
\item Port number
\item Domain name
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A MAC address is a hardware address used at the data link layer.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
A router forwards packets based on:

\begin{qoptions}
\item MAC address
\item IP address
\item Port number
\item VLAN ID
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Routers use IP addresses to make forwarding decisions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
What is a subnet mask used for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item MAC address
\item IP address class
\item Network and host portions of an IP address
\item DNS server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} A subnet mask divides an IP address into network and host portions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
The OSI model layer closest to the physical medium is:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Data link
\item Physical
\item Network
\item Transport
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The physical layer deals with bits, cables, and signals.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
TCP is considered reliable because it uses:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Sequence numbers and acknowledgments
\item Broadcasting
\item No connection setup
\item Fixed ports only
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} TCP uses sequence numbers, acknowledgments, and retransmissions for reliability.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
UDP is preferred for:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Reliable file transfer
\item Real-time streaming and low-latency applications
\item Email delivery
\item Web browsing requiring guaranteed delivery
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} UDP low overhead and lack of retransmission make it suitable for real-time traffic.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
DNS primarily uses:

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP port 53
\item UDP port 53
\item TCP port 80
\item UDP port 161
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} DNS queries primarily use UDP port 53.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
A Layer 3 switch combines functions of a:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Hub and repeater
\item Switch and router
\item Firewall and IDS
\item Modem and gateway
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Layer 3 switches perform both Layer 2 switching and Layer 3 routing.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: has seven layers?

\begin{qoptions}
\item OSI model
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item Presentation layer
\item SMTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} OSI model refers to the concept where has seven layers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 29} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: has four layers?

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP/IP standard model
\item TCP
\item ICMP
\item DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} TCP/IP standard model refers to the concept where has four layers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 30} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: has five layers?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Physical layer
\item VLAN
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item Data link layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} TCP/IP equivalent model refers to the concept where has five layers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 31} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: transmits raw bits over a medium?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Hub
\item Physical layer
\item Application layer
\item SMTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Physical layer refers to the concept where transmits raw bits over a medium.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 32} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: handles framing and MAC addressing?

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP/IP standard model
\item DNS
\item Data link layer
\item FTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Data link layer refers to the concept where handles framing and MAC addressing.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 33} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: handles logical addressing and routing?

\begin{qoptions}
\item FTP
\item DHCP
\item Network layer
\item UDP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Network layer refers to the concept where handles logical addressing and routing.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 34} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides end-to-end communication and reliability?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Data link layer
\item Session layer
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item Transport layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Transport layer refers to the concept where provides end-to-end communication and reliability.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 35} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: manages application dialogs?

\begin{qoptions}
\item UDP
\item Session layer
\item Transport layer
\item Presentation layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Session layer refers to the concept where manages application dialogs.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 36} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: handles encryption and data format conversion?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Presentation layer
\item Hub
\item ARP
\item IPv4 address
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Presentation layer refers to the concept where handles encryption and data format conversion.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 37} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides network services to applications?

\begin{qoptions}
\item FTP
\item Application layer
\item SSH
\item Data link layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Application layer refers to the concept where provides network services to applications.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 38} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is connection-oriented and reliable?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Telnet
\item FTP
\item HTTP
\item TCP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} TCP refers to the concept where is connection-oriented and reliable.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 39} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is connectionless and has low overhead?

\begin{qoptions}
\item RFC 1918
\item ICMP
\item UDP
\item Session layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} UDP refers to the concept where is connectionless and has low overhead.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 40} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP ports 20 and 21?

\begin{qoptions}
\item FTP
\item IPv4 address
\item ICMP
\item UDP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} FTP refers to the concept where uses TCP ports 20 and 21.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 41} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP port 80?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Telnet
\item HTTP
\item IPv4 address
\item SMTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} HTTP refers to the concept where uses TCP port 80.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 42} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP port 443?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ICMP
\item TCP/IP standard model
\item HTTPS
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} HTTPS refers to the concept where uses TCP port 443.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 43} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP port 22?

\begin{qoptions}
\item SSH
\item Network layer
\item Router
\item Transport layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} SSH refers to the concept where uses TCP port 22.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 44} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP port 23 and is insecure?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Telnet
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item FTP
\item Transport layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Telnet refers to the concept where uses TCP port 23 and is insecure.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 45} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: primarily uses UDP port 53?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Network layer
\item Application layer
\item DNS
\item ARP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} DNS refers to the concept where primarily uses UDP port 53.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 46} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses TCP port 25?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Switch
\item SMTP
\item SSH
\item Hub
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} SMTP refers to the concept where uses TCP port 25.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 47} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: dynamically assigns IP addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Presentation layer
\item HTTP
\item DHCP
\item SSH
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} DHCP refers to the concept where dynamically assigns IP addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 48} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: maps IP addresses to MAC addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ICMP
\item TCP
\item Telnet
\item ARP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} ARP refers to the concept where maps IP addresses to MAC addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 49} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides error reporting and diagnostics?

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP
\item Network layer
\item IPv6 address
\item ICMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} ICMP refers to the concept where provides error reporting and diagnostics.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 50} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 32 bits long?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Switch
\item TCP/IP standard model
\item IPv4 address
\item HTTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} IPv4 address refers to the concept where is 32 bits long.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 51} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 128 bits long?

\begin{qoptions}
\item RFC 1918
\item IPv4 address
\item STP
\item IPv6 address
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} IPv6 address refers to the concept where is 128 bits long.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 52} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is a Layer 2 hardware address?

\begin{qoptions}
\item MAC address
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item VLAN
\item Telnet
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} MAC address refers to the concept where is a Layer 2 hardware address.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 53} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: operates at Layer 2 and forwards frames by MAC?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Data link layer
\item Switch
\item DHCP
\item DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Switch refers to the concept where operates at Layer 2 and forwards frames by MAC.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 54} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: operates at Layer 3 and forwards packets by IP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VLAN
\item DHCP
\item SMTP
\item Router
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Router refers to the concept where operates at Layer 3 and forwards packets by IP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 55} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: operates at Layer 1 and repeats signals?

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP/IP equivalent model
\item MAC address
\item Session layer
\item Hub
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Hub refers to the concept where operates at Layer 1 and repeats signals.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 56} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: logically segments a network into broadcast domains?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VLAN
\item TCP
\item SSH
\item DHCP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VLAN refers to the concept where logically segments a network into broadcast domains.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 57} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: prevents Layer 2 loops?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Application layer
\item DNS
\item HTTP
\item STP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} STP refers to the concept where prevents Layer 2 loops.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 58} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Fundamentals}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: defines private IPv4 address ranges?

\begin{qoptions}
\item MAC address
\item Data link layer
\item DNS
\item RFC 1918
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} RFC 1918 refers to the concept where defines private IPv4 address ranges.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{OSI and TCP/IP Models}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: OSI and TCP/IP Models}{singlecol}
How many layers does the OSI reference model have?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 4
\item 5
\item 7
\item 9
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} The OSI model has seven layers: physical, data link, network, transport, session, presentation, and application.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: OSI and TCP/IP Models}{singlecol}
How many layers does the TCP/IP standard model have?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 4
\item 5
\item 6
\item 7
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The TCP/IP standard model has four layers: network interface, internet, transport, and application.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: OSI and TCP/IP Models}{singlecol}
Which layer is responsible for end-to-end communication and flow control?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Network layer
\item Transport layer
\item Data link layer
\item Physical layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The transport layer provides end-to-end communication, reliability, and flow control (e.g., TCP).
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: OSI and TCP/IP Models}{singlecol}
Which layer handles logical addressing and routing?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Transport layer
\item Network layer
\item Session layer
\item Presentation layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The network layer uses logical addresses (IP) and routes packets between networks.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: OSI and TCP/IP Models}{singlecol}
Which layer is responsible for framing, MAC addressing, and error detection on a local link?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Network layer
\item Transport layer
\item Data link layer
\item Application layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} The data link layer handles framing, MAC addresses, and local link error detection.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Network Protocols}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Protocols}{singlecol}
Which protocol uses ports 20 and 21 for file transfer?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HTTP
\item FTP
\item SMTP
\item DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} FTP uses TCP port 21 for control and port 20 for data transfer.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Protocols}{singlecol}
Which protocol resolves domain names to IP addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DHCP
\item DNS
\item SNMP
\item NTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} DNS (Domain Name System) maps human-readable domain names to IP addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Protocols}{singlecol}
Which transport protocol is connection-oriented and reliable?

\begin{qoptions}
\item UDP
\item TCP
\item ICMP
\item IP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} TCP provides connection-oriented, reliable, ordered delivery with flow and congestion control.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Protocols}{singlecol}
Which transport protocol is connectionless and has low overhead?

\begin{qoptions}
\item TCP
\item UDP
\item HTTP
\item FTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} UDP is connectionless, offers low latency and overhead, but does not guarantee delivery.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Network Devices}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Devices}{singlecol}
Which device operates at Layer 2 and forwards frames based on MAC addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Router
\item Switch
\item Hub
\item Firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A switch operates at the data link layer and forwards frames using MAC address tables.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Devices}{singlecol}
Which device operates at Layer 3 and forwards packets based on IP addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Switch
\item Router
\item Hub
\item Bridge
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A router operates at the network layer and makes forwarding decisions based on IP routing tables.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Network Devices}{singlecol}
Which device simply repeats signals to all connected ports?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Switch
\item Router
\item Hub
\item Firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} A hub is a Layer 1 device that broadcasts signals to all connected devices.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Enterprise Security Threats}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack sends an overwhelming amount of traffic from many sources?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DoS
\item DDoS
\item Phishing
\item Spoofing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} DDoS uses multiple distributed sources to flood a target.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack falsifies the source address of packets?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Phishing
\item Spoofing
\item Sniffing
\item Spamming
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Spoofing impersonates a trusted source by falsifying addresses or identities.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which type of malware replicates itself across networks without user action?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Virus
\item Worm
\item Trojan
\item Spyware
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Worms can self-replicate and spread across networks, often without user interaction.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which malware disguises itself as legitimate software?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Virus
\item Worm
\item Trojan
\item Adware
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} A Trojan horse masquerades as legitimate software to trick users into executing it.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Ransomware primarily does what?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Steals credentials
\item Encrypts data and demands payment
\item Deletes logs
\item Monitors traffic
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Ransomware encrypts files and demands a ransom for decryption.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack intercepts communications between two parties without their knowledge?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Man-in-the-middle
\item DoS
\item Phishing
\item Tailgating
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} A man-in-the-middle attack intercepts and possibly alters communication between parties.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which protocol can help prevent man-in-the-middle attacks by verifying server identity?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HTTP
\item HTTPS
\item FTP
\item Telnet
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} HTTPS uses TLS/SSL to encrypt traffic and authenticate the server.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which wireless security protocol is more secure than WEP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item WPA2/WPA3
\item WEP
\item WPA (TKIP only)
\item Open network
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} WPA2 and WPA3 provide stronger encryption and authentication than WEP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
What is a DMZ typically used for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Hosting internal databases only
\item Hosting public-facing servers
\item User workstations
\item Printer sharing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A DMZ hosts public-facing services between the Internet and internal network.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which security zone usually has the highest priority in Huawei firewalls?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Untrust
\item Trust
\item DMZ
\item Local
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} The Local zone (firewall itself) has priority 100, the highest default priority.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which practice limits the damage from compromised accounts?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Least privilege
\item Sharing admin passwords
\item Disabling logs
\item Open shares
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Least privilege gives users only the access they need, limiting blast radius.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
What is the purpose of host-based intrusion prevention?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Route packets
\item Detect and block malicious host activity
\item Assign IP addresses
\item Synchronize time
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Host-based IPS monitors and blocks suspicious activity on an endpoint.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which technology collects and analyzes security logs centrally?

\begin{qoptions}
\item SIEM
\item SNMP
\item DHCP
\item ARP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} SIEM centralizes log collection and analysis.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
What is a key benefit of centralized security management?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Reduced visibility
\item Unified policy and monitoring
\item More complex passwords
\item Faster malware spread
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Centralized management provides unified visibility, policy control, and monitoring.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
What is an insider threat?

\begin{qoptions}
\item An attack from outside the network
\item A security risk originating from within the organization
\item A hardware vendor
\item A natural disaster
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Insider threats come from employees, contractors, or partners with internal access.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack sends oversized ICMP packets to crash a target?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Ping of death
\item Ping sweep
\item ARP spoofing
\item DNS hijacking
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Ping of death sends ICMP packets larger than the maximum allowed size.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack uses broadcast pings to amplify traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Smurf attack
\item SYN flood
\item Fraggle
\item Session hijacking
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Smurf attacks send ICMP echo requests to broadcast addresses with a spoofed source.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which attack intercepts ARP messages to redirect traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ARP spoofing
\item DNS spoofing
\item IP spoofing
\item MAC flooding
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} ARP spoofing sends forged ARP replies to associate an attacker MAC with another IP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
A SYN flood is a type of:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Denial of Service attack
\item Phishing attack
\item Data exfiltration
\item Physical attack
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} SYN floods exhaust server connection resources with half-open TCP connections.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
ARP spoofing is used to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Intercept traffic on a local network
\item Encrypt files
\item Speed up routing
\item Authenticate users
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Attackers use forged ARP messages to redirect local traffic through themselves.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
The principle of least privilege aims to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Give everyone admin rights
\item Limit user access to the minimum necessary
\item Disable all logging
\item Remove firewalls
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Least privilege reduces risk by restricting access to only what is needed.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
A SIEM system is primarily used for:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Centralized log analysis and threat detection
\item Assigning IP addresses
\item Switching frames
\item Manufacturing cables
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} SIEM aggregates and analyzes security events and logs.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: monitors traffic without altering it?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Ransomware
\item Passive attack
\item Ping of death
\item DoS attack
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Passive attack refers to the concept where monitors traffic without altering it.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: modifies or disrupts systems or data?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Active attack
\item Ping of death
\item Man-in-the-middle attack
\item DDoS attack
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Active attack refers to the concept where modifies or disrupts systems or data.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: includes viruses, worms, Trojans, and ransomware?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Malware
\item Worm
\item Virus
\item Social engineering
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Malware refers to the concept where includes viruses, worms, Trojans, and ransomware.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: requires a host program to spread?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Active attack
\item Man-in-the-middle attack
\item Virus
\item DMZ
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Virus refers to the concept where requires a host program to spread.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: can self-replicate across networks?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DDoS attack
\item DoS attack
\item Worm
\item ARP spoofing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Worm refers to the concept where can self-replicate across networks.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}

\item Trojan

\end{qcard}

\textbf{Concept:} Ransomware refers to the concept where encrypts data and demands payment.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 33} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Enterprise Security Threats}{singlecol}

\begin{qoptions}
\item Passive attack

\textbf{Concept:} SIEM refers to the concept where centralizes log collection and analysis.
