\section{Communication Network Security}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Communication Network Security}{singlecol}
Which threat involves intercepting data traveling across a network?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Eavesdropping
\item DoS
\item Phishing
\item Tailgating
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Eavesdropping intercepts network communications to steal information.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Zone Border Security}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Zone Border Security}{singlecol}
What is the main purpose of a security zone?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Increase Internet speed
\item Group interfaces with similar security levels
\item Replace antivirus
\item Disable routing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Security zones group network interfaces with similar trust levels to enforce inter-zone policies.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Zone Border Security}{singlecol}
Which device is typically deployed at a zone border to enforce access control?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Hub
\item Firewall
\item Repeater
\item Bridge
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Firewalls are deployed at network boundaries to enforce security policies between zones.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Computing Environment Security}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Computing Environment Security}{singlecol}
Which control helps protect endpoints from malware?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Antivirus/EDR
\item Physical locks only
\item Hub replacement
\item Disabling DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Antivirus and endpoint detection and response (EDR) protect hosts from malware and suspicious behavior.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Firewall Basics}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which firewall feature inspects application-layer data to detect attacks?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Packet filtering
\item Application inspection
\item Hub forwarding
\item NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Application inspection examines payload content for malicious patterns.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which Huawei firewall command mode is used for system-level configuration?

\begin{qoptions}
\item User view
\item System view
\item Interface view
\item Policy view
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} System view allows global-level configuration on Huawei devices.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which view is entered after the system-view command?

\begin{qoptions}
\item User view
\item System view
\item Interface view
\item None
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The system-view command enters system view from user view.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What is the purpose of a security profile in NGFW?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Physical installation
\item Applying advanced security features such as IPS/AV/URL filtering
\item Layer 1 forwarding
\item DHCP relay
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Security profiles group advanced inspection features applied to policies.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which firewall generation combines traditional firewall functions with application awareness and intrusion prevention?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\item Next-generation firewall
\item Stateful firewall
\item Proxy firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Next-generation firewalls integrate application awareness, IPS, antivirus, and user identity.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What is the purpose of a default security policy deny action?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Improve performance
\item Ensure least-privilege access
\item Enable logging
\item Allow management
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Default deny enforces least privilege by blocking unspecified traffic.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which zone would typically contain public web servers?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Trust
\item Untrust
\item DMZ
\item Local
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Public-facing servers are placed in the DMZ for isolation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What does policy hit counting help with?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Routing optimization
\item Verifying policy usage and troubleshooting
\item MAC learning
\item DHCP assignment
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Hit counts show which policies are triggered, aiding auditing and troubleshooting.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which of the following is NOT a firewall deployment mode?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Routing mode
\item Transparent mode
\item Hybrid mode
\item Broadcast mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Firewalls deploy in routing, transparent (bridge), or hybrid modes; broadcast mode is not standard.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What happens when traffic moves from a higher-priority zone to a lower-priority zone?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Inbound traffic
\item Outbound traffic
\item No traffic allowed
\item Always dropped
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Traffic from a higher-priority zone to a lower-priority zone is considered outbound.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What is the priority range for custom security zones on Huawei firewalls?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 0-50
\item 1-100
\item 1-99 excluding defaults
\item 0-100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Custom zone priorities range from 1 to 99, avoiding reserved default values.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which action in a security policy can log permitted or denied traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Permit
\item Deny
\item Both permit and deny
\item Neither
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Security policies can enable logging for both permit and deny actions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which security policy action is typically applied to suspicious traffic for further inspection?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Permit
\item Deny
\item Block
\item Redirect
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Suspicious traffic is commonly denied or blocked by policy.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
When a return packet arrives, what does a stateful firewall check first?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Routing table
\item Session table
\item MAC table
\item ARP table
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} A stateful firewall first checks the session table to find a matching connection.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
What happens if a return packet does not match any session table entry?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Forwarded normally
\item Dropped or inspected against policies
\item Always permitted
\item Sent to DMZ
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Without a matching session, the firewall treats the packet as a new connection and applies policies.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which protocol commonly requires ASPF support due to dynamic port negotiation?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HTTP
\item FTP
\item DNS
\item NTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} FTP negotiates dynamic data ports in its control channel, requiring ASPF.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
ASPF primarily inspects traffic at which layer?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Network layer only
\item Application layer
\item Physical layer
\item Data link layer
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ASPF performs application-layer inspection to identify negotiated connections.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
In Huawei firewalls, the default priority of Trust is:

\begin{qoptions}
\item 5
\item 50
\item 85
\item 100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Trust zone default priority is 85.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Traffic from a low-priority zone to a high-priority zone is considered:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Outbound
\item Inbound
\item Blocked by default
\item Always permitted
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Traffic entering a higher-priority zone from a lower-priority zone is inbound.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
The default security policy action is:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Permit
\item Deny
\item Log
\item Redirect
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Huawei firewalls deny traffic by default unless explicitly permitted.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Stateful inspection relies on:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Session tables
\item MAC tables only
\item DNS records
\item DHCP leases
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Session tables track connection state for stateful inspection.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
ASPF helps firewalls handle:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Multi-channel protocols
\item Static routing
\item MAC learning
\item DHCP relay
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} ASPF inspects protocols that negotiate dynamic secondary channels.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 85 by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Security policy logging
\item Security zones
\item Trust zone priority
\item Hybrid mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Trust zone priority refers to the concept where is 85 by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 50 by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item DMZ zone priority
\item Hybrid mode
\item Intra-zone traffic
\item Routing mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} DMZ zone priority refers to the concept where is 50 by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 5 by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Untrust zone priority
\item DMZ zone priority
\item ASPF
\item Policy hit count
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Untrust zone priority refers to the concept where is 5 by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is 100 by default?

\begin{qoptions}
\item ASPF
\item Hybrid mode
\item Policy hit count
\item Local zone priority
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Local zone priority refers to the concept where is 100 by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is deny?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Trust zone priority
\item Routing mode
\item Default security policy action
\item Transparent mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Default security policy action refers to the concept where is deny.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: tracks connections in a session table?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Stateful firewall
\item Untrust zone priority
\item Routing mode
\item DMZ zone priority
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Stateful firewall refers to the concept where tracks connections in a session table.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 29} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: inspects packets individually without state?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Trust zone priority
\item Routing mode
\item Security zones
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Packet-filtering firewall refers to the concept where inspects packets individually without state.
\end{explainbox}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 30} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: inspects multi-channel protocols like FTP?

\item ASPF
\item Local zone priority
\item Stateful firewall

\textbf{Concept:} ASPF refers to the concept where inspects multi-channel protocols like FTP.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 31} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}

\begin{qoptions}
\item Local zone priority
\item Next-generation firewall
\item Security policy logging
\item Untrust zone priority
\end{qoptions}

\textbf{Concept:} Next-generation firewall refers to the concept where includes application awareness and IPS.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 32} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}

\begin{qoptions}
\item Security policy logging
\item Next-generation firewall
\item Untrust zone priority
\item Security zones
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Security zones refers to the concept where group interfaces with similar trust levels.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 33} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: can be controlled by policies?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Trust zone priority
\item Next-generation firewall
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\item Intra-zone traffic
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Intra-zone traffic refers to the concept where can be controlled by policies.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 34} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: helps verify which rules are used?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Untrust zone priority
\item Intra-zone traffic
\item Policy hit count
\item DMZ zone priority
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Policy hit count refers to the concept where helps verify which rules are used.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 35} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: can be enabled for permit and deny actions?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Routing mode
\item Security policy logging
\item ASPF
\item Security zones
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Security policy logging refers to the concept where can be enabled for permit and deny actions.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 36} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is a firewall deployment mode?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Security zones
\item Policy hit count
\item Routing mode
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Routing mode refers to the concept where is a firewall deployment mode.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 37} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is a firewall deployment mode?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Trust zone priority
\item Security zones
\item Transparent mode
\item Stateful firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Transparent mode refers to the concept where is a firewall deployment mode.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 38} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Basics}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: is a firewall deployment mode?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Next-generation firewall
\item Intra-zone traffic
\item Hybrid mode
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Hybrid mode refers to the concept where is a firewall deployment mode.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Security Zones}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Zones}{singlecol}
What is the default priority of the Trust zone on a Huawei firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 5
\item 50
\item 85
\item 100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} The Trust zone has a default priority of 85.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Zones}{singlecol}
What is the default priority of the DMZ zone on a Huawei firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 5
\item 50
\item 85
\item 100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The DMZ zone has a default priority of 50.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Zones}{singlecol}
What is the default priority of the Untrust zone on a Huawei firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 5
\item 50
\item 85
\item 100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The Untrust zone has a default priority of 5.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Zones}{singlecol}
What is the default priority of the Local zone on a Huawei firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 5
\item 50
\item 85
\item 100
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} The Local zone represents the firewall itself and has the highest default priority of 100.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Security Policies}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Policies}{singlecol}
What is the default action of a Huawei firewall security policy if no rule matches?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Permit
\item Deny
\item Log only
\item Redirect
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} By default, unmatched traffic is denied.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Security Policies}{singlecol}
Which components can be used as matching conditions in a security policy? (single best answer)

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source/destination zone, address, service, application, user, time
\item Only source IP
\item Only destination port
\item Only protocol
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Security policies can match on zones, addresses, users, services, applications, and time.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Stateful Inspection}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Stateful Inspection}{singlecol}
What is a session table used for in a stateful firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Storing user passwords
\item Tracking established connections
\item Routing packets
\item Encrypting traffic
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The session table tracks connection state so return traffic can be permitted without a new policy lookup.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Stateful Inspection}{singlecol}
Which type of firewall inspects packets individually without connection state?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Stateful firewall
\item Packet-filtering firewall
\item Proxy firewall
\item Next-generation firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Packet-filtering firewalls inspect each packet independently based on header information.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{ASPF}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: ASPF}{singlecol}
What does ASPF stand for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Advanced Stateful Packet Filtering
\item Application Specific Packet Filtering
\item Automatic Security Policy Forwarding
\item Advanced Stateful Policy Firewall
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} ASPF is Application Specific Packet Filtering, used to inspect multi-channel protocols.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: ASPF}{singlecol}
Why is ASPF needed for protocols like FTP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item FTP only uses one port
\item FTP opens dynamic data channels
\item FTP encrypts all traffic
\item FTP does not use TCP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} FTP opens a dynamic data channel, and ASPF inspects the control channel to permit the negotiated data channel.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{NAT}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type is also called many-to-one NAT?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Static NAT
\item Dynamic NAT
\item NAPT
\item NAT Server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} NAPT maps multiple private addresses to one public IP using port numbers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type preserves a one-to-one mapping between public and private addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Static NAT
\item NAPT
\item Easy-IP
\item Dynamic PAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Static NAT maps one public IP to one private IP consistently.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type uses the outbound interface IP as the public address?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAPT
\item NAT No-PAT
\item Easy-IP
\item Destination NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Easy-IP uses the firewall egress interface IP for translation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which source NAT mode maps one private address to one public address without port translation?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAPT
\item NAT No-PAT
\item Easy-IP
\item NAT Server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT No-PAT performs one-to-one address translation without changing port numbers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
When many internal hosts access the Internet using one public IP, which NAT is used?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT No-PAT
\item NAPT
\item NAT Server
\item Static NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAPT allows many-to-one address translation using port numbers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Destination NAT is also known as:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Static NAT
\item Port forwarding / virtual IP
\item PAT
\item Proxy ARP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Destination NAT maps external destinations to internal hosts, commonly called port forwarding or virtual IP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type maps a public IP/port to a private server IP/port?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item NAT Server
\item NAPT
\item Bidirectional NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT Server publishes internal services to external networks.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Bidirectional NAT is useful when:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Only source address translation is needed
\item Both source and destination must be rewritten
\item No NAT is needed
\item Only DNS traffic is involved
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} It translates both source and destination addresses in a single flow.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which problem can occur if NAT ALG is disabled for FTP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item FTP control channel fails
\item FTP data channel may fail because dynamic ports are not opened
\item FTP becomes encrypted
\item FTP speeds up
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Without ALG, the firewall cannot open the negotiated FTP data channel.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which address type is preserved by NAT No-PAT?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source port
\item Destination port
\item Both source and destination ports
\item No ports
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT No-PAT translates only IP addresses, preserving port numbers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
What is a major benefit of NAPT?

\begin{qoptions}
\item One-to-one address mapping
\item Conserves public IP addresses
\item Full protocol transparency
\item No ALG needed
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAPT conserves public IPs by sharing one address across many internal hosts.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
A NAT Server entry is typically:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Bidirectional and persistent
\item Temporary and random
\item Encrypted
\item Only for outbound traffic
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT Server entries are configured statically and persist for inbound access.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
NAT ALG is often enabled together with:

\begin{qoptions}
\item ASPF
\item Routing
\item DHCP
\item DNS
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} ALG and ASPF work together to inspect dynamic application channels.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which RFC defines private IPv4 address space?

\begin{qoptions}
\item RFC 1918
\item RFC 2328
\item RFC 793
\item RFC 959
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} RFC 1918 defines the private IPv4 address ranges.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Hairpin NAT allows internal users to access an internal server using:

\begin{qoptions}
\item The server private IP only
\item The server public NAT address
\item A VPN tunnel
\item Broadcast address
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Hairpin NAT lets internal hosts reach an internal server via its public NAT address.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
NAPT is also known as:

\begin{qoptions}
\item PAT
\item Static NAT
\item NAT Server
\item Bridge NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Port Address Translation (PAT) is another name for NAPT.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Static NAT is most often used when:

\begin{qoptions}
\item A server must always use the same public IP
\item Many hosts share one IP
\item Ports must be preserved
\item Outbound only traffic exists
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Static NAT provides a consistent one-to-one public-to-private mapping.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Destination NAT is commonly used to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Allow internal hosts to browse the web
\item Publish internal services to external users
\item Encrypt VPN traffic
\item Assign IP addresses
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Destination NAT maps external destinations to internal servers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which feature rewrites IP addresses inside application payloads?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT ALG
\item NAPT
\item Static NAT
\item Route lookup
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT ALG handles protocols that embed address information in payloads.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: translates IP addresses between private and public?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT
\item NAPT
\item Easy-IP
\item NAT Server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT refers to the concept where translates IP addresses between private and public.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: translates the source IP of outbound packets?

\begin{qoptions}
\item RFC 1918
\item NAT Server
\item Static NAT
\item Source NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Source NAT refers to the concept where translates the source IP of outbound packets.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: translates the destination IP of inbound packets?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAPT
\item NAT No-PAT
\item Static NAT
\item Destination NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Destination NAT refers to the concept where translates the destination IP of inbound packets.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: publishes internal services to the Internet?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT Server
\item Destination NAT
\item RFC 1918
\item Static NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT Server refers to the concept where publishes internal services to the Internet.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: translates both source and destination addresses?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Static NAT
\item NAT
\item Bidirectional NAT
\item Destination NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Bidirectional NAT refers to the concept where translates both source and destination addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: translates many private addresses to one public IP using ports?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item NAPT
\item NAT
\item NAT ALG
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAPT refers to the concept where translates many private addresses to one public IP using ports.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: performs one-to-one address translation preserving ports?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Destination NAT
\item Easy-IP
\item RFC 1918
\item NAT No-PAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT No-PAT refers to the concept where performs one-to-one address translation preserving ports.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: uses the outbound interface IP for translation?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item NAPT
\item Easy-IP
\item Hairpin NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Easy-IP refers to the concept where uses the outbound interface IP for translation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: rewrites addresses inside application payloads?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item NAT ALG
\item NAT
\item Static NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT ALG refers to the concept where rewrites addresses inside application payloads.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 29} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: defines private IPv4 address ranges?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Destination NAT
\item Static NAT
\item NAT ALG
\item RFC 1918
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} RFC 1918 refers to the concept where defines private IPv4 address ranges.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 30} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: lets internal users access servers via public NAT address?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT
\item Hairpin NAT
\item NAT No-PAT
\item Destination NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Hairpin NAT refers to the concept where lets internal users access servers via public NAT address.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 31} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides a persistent one-to-one mapping?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Static NAT
\item NAT
\item RFC 1918
\item Bidirectional NAT
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Static NAT refers to the concept where provides a persistent one-to-one mapping.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{NAT Basics}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT Basics}{singlecol}
What does NAT primarily translate?

\begin{qoptions}
\item MAC addresses
\item IP addresses
\item Port numbers only
\item Domain names
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT translates IP addresses, typically between private and public addresses.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Source NAT}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Source NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type translates the source IP address of outgoing packets?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item Destination NAT
\item Bidirectional NAT
\item NAT Server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Source NAT changes the source IP so internal hosts can use public addresses to access the Internet.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Source NAT}{singlecol}
What is the main purpose of NAPT?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Translate many private addresses to one public IP using port multiplexing
\item Hide the destination IP
\item Encrypt traffic
\item Provide VPN access
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} NAPT (Network Address and Port Translation) maps multiple private addresses to a single public IP using different ports.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Destination NAT}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Destination NAT}{singlecol}
Which NAT type translates the destination IP address of incoming packets?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Source NAT
\item Destination NAT
\item Bidirectional NAT
\item NAT Server
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Destination NAT rewrites the destination IP, often to expose internal servers to external users.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{NAT Server}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT Server}{singlecol}
What is a NAT Server used for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Hiding internal server IP addresses
\item Publishing internal services to the Internet
\item Encrypting server traffic
\item Load balancing only
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT Server maps public IPs/ports to private server IPs/ports so external users can access internal services.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{NAT ALG}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: NAT ALG}{singlecol}
What is the purpose of NAT ALG?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Encrypt NAT traffic
\item Translate application-layer addresses embedded in protocol payloads
\item Replace firewalls
\item Assign IP addresses
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} NAT Application Level Gateway inspects and rewrites IP addresses inside application-layer protocols (e.g., FTP, SIP).
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Firewall Hot Standby}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
In active/standby mode, how many firewalls forward traffic at the same time?

\begin{qoptions}
\item One
\item Two
\item All
\item None
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} In active/standby mode, one firewall actively forwards while the other remains standby.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
In load-sharing mode, how many firewalls forward traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item One
\item Two or more
\item None
\item Only the backup
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Load-sharing mode allows multiple firewalls to forward traffic simultaneously.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
What is the default VRRP advertisement interval?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 1 second
\item 3 seconds
\item 10 seconds
\item 30 seconds
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP master sends advertisements approximately every 1 second by default.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which VRRP priority value is highest configurable?

\begin{qoptions}
\item 1
\item 100
\item 254
\item 255
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP priority ranges from 1 to 254 configurable; 255 is reserved for the IP owner.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
What happens if a backup router stops receiving VRRP advertisements?

\begin{qoptions}
\item It remains backup
\item It preempts and becomes master
\item It shuts down
\item It sends ARP requests
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Missing advertisements trigger a backup to assume the master role.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
What does VGMP group priority determine?

\begin{qoptions}
\item NAT pool size
\item Which firewall becomes active
\item Number of interfaces
\item DHCP lease time
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} VGMP priority determines the active/standby role among redundant firewalls.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which HRP state indicates the firewall is currently forwarding traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Standby
\item Active
\item Initialize
\item Backup
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The active HRP peer forwards traffic and synchronizes state to the standby.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which HRP command enables automatic configuration synchronization?

\begin{qoptions}
\item hrp enable
\item hrp auto-sync config
\item hrp mirror session
\item hrp vrrp enable
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} hrp auto-sync config enables automatic configuration synchronization between peers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which deployment places both firewalls inline with the same traffic path?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Dual-hot standby
\item Active/standby inline
\item Route backup only
\item Transparent bridge
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Inline active/standby deployment places firewalls in the same forwarding path.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
What is a common requirement for HRP heartbeat links?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Use the same interface as user traffic
\item Use a dedicated high-reliability link
\item Use wireless link only
\item No link required
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Heartbeat links should be dedicated and reliable to avoid false failovers.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which mechanism reduces unnecessary VRRP state changes when interface flaps briefly?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Preempt delay
\item Track interface
\item Authentication
\item Priority 255
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Preempt delay prevents rapid master/backup flapping.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which HRP feature ensures sessions survive a failover?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Session backup
\item Route backup
\item NAT ALG
\item URL filtering
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} HRP session backup keeps session state synchronized so connections continue after failover.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
VRRP master election is based primarily on:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Priority value
\item Router hostname
\item Number of interfaces
\item Uptime only
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The router with the highest priority becomes the VRRP master.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
VGMP group management prevents:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Split-brain VRRP states
\item NAT exhaustion
\item DNS failures
\item DHCP conflicts
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VGMP ensures VRRP groups fail over consistently.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
HRP is used to synchronize:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Session and configuration data
\item Email messages
\item Web pages
\item Printer drivers
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} HRP synchronizes sessions, NAT, server-map entries, and configurations.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
In active/standby mode, the standby firewall:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Does not forward traffic until failover
\item Forwards half the traffic
\item Processes all traffic
\item Acts as a switch
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} The standby firewall remains ready but inactive until failover.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: provides gateway redundancy with a virtual IP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP
\item Load-sharing mode
\item Active/standby mode
\item VRRP master
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP refers to the concept where provides gateway redundancy with a virtual IP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: forwards traffic for the virtual IP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP
\item VRRP backup
\item HRP session backup
\item VRRP master
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP master refers to the concept where forwards traffic for the virtual IP.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 19} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: takes over if the master fails?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP master
\item VRRP backup
\item Load-sharing mode
\item Active/standby mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP backup refers to the concept where takes over if the master fails.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 20} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: manages VRRP group states consistently?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Load-sharing mode
\item Preempt delay
\item VRRP
\item VGMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} VGMP refers to the concept where manages VRRP group states consistently.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: synchronizes sessions and configurations?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Active/standby mode
\item HRP
\item Heartbeat link
\item VRRP master
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} HRP refers to the concept where synchronizes sessions and configurations.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: has one firewall forwarding traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Preempt delay
\item Active/standby mode
\item Load-sharing mode
\item VGMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Active/standby mode refers to the concept where has one firewall forwarding traffic.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: has multiple firewalls forwarding traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP backup
\item Load-sharing mode
\item Heartbeat link
\item VGMP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Load-sharing mode refers to the concept where has multiple firewalls forwarding traffic.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: should be dedicated and reliable?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP master
\item HRP
\item VGMP
\item Heartbeat link
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Heartbeat link refers to the concept where should be dedicated and reliable.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: ensures connections survive failover?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HRP session backup
\item VGMP
\item Preempt delay
\item Active/standby mode
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} HRP session backup refers to the concept where ensures connections survive failover.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Firewall Hot Standby}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: reduces rapid master-backup flapping?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Load-sharing mode
\item HRP session backup
\item VRRP backup
\item Preempt delay
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Preempt delay refers to the concept where reduces rapid master-backup flapping.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{VRRP}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: VRRP}{singlecol}
What does VRRP stand for?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Virtual Router Redundancy Protocol
\item Virtual Routing Resolution Protocol
\item Virtual Redundant Routing Path
\item Virtual Routing Relay Protocol
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VRRP provides gateway redundancy by allowing multiple routers to share a virtual IP address.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: VRRP}{singlecol}
In VRRP, which router forwards traffic for the virtual IP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Backup router
\item Master router
\item All routers simultaneously
\item Only the physical IP owner
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The VRRP master router owns the virtual IP and forwards traffic; backups take over if it fails.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{VGMP}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: VGMP}{singlecol}
What does VGMP manage on Huawei firewalls?

\begin{qoptions}
\item VRRP groups
\item NAT pools
\item Routing tables
\item DHCP scopes
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} VGMP (VRRP Group Management Protocol) manages the state of multiple VRRP groups consistently.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{HRP}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: HRP}{singlecol}
What is the primary purpose of HRP?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Encrypt traffic
\item Synchronize sessions and configurations between firewalls
\item Assign IP addresses
\item Filter URLs
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} HRP (Huawei Redundancy Protocol) synchronizes sessions, configurations, and state between hot-standby firewalls.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\section{Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}
\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 1} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which phase of an attack involves identifying vulnerable services?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Exploitation
\item Reconnaissance
\item Installation
\item Exfiltration
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Reconnaissance gathers information about targets before exploitation.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 2} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which phase involves gaining unauthorized access?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Reconnaissance
\item Exploitation
\item Covering tracks
\item Reporting
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Exploitation uses vulnerabilities to gain access to the target.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 3} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which deployment mode allows IDS to monitor traffic without affecting it?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Inline
\item Tap/SPAN
\item Bridge
\item Gateway
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} IDS commonly connects to a tap or switch SPAN port for passive monitoring.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 4} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which signature action drops the offending packet?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Alert
\item Block
\item Permit
\item Mirror
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Block actions discard malicious packets.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 5} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
What is a false positive in IPS?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Attack missed
\item Legitimate traffic blocked as malicious
\item Correct detection
\item Signature update failure
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} False positives incorrectly classify legitimate traffic as malicious.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 6} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
What is a false negative in IPS?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Legitimate traffic blocked
\item Attack missed by IPS
\item Correct block
\item Logging error
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} False negatives occur when an actual attack is not detected.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 7} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which malware type encrypts user files and demands ransom?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Virus
\item Worm
\item Trojan
\item Ransomware
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} Ransomware encrypts files and demands a ransom for decryption.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 8} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which approach only allows known-good applications to run?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Blacklisting
\item Whitelisting
\item Heuristics
\item Sandboxing
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Whitelisting permits only approved applications.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 9} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which technique detects malware by examining code behavior rather than signatures?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Signature scanning
\item Heuristic analysis
\item Whitelisting
\item Patching
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Heuristics analyze code behavior to identify potentially malicious activity.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 10} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which protocol is commonly targeted by brute-force attacks against remote access?

\begin{qoptions}
\item HTTP
\item SSH
\item DNS
\item NTP
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} SSH and similar remote-access protocols are frequent brute-force targets.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 11} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
What does an IPS signature database contain?

\begin{qoptions}
\item User passwords
\item Known attack patterns
\item Routing tables
\item DNS records
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Signature databases contain patterns used to identify known attacks.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 12} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
What is the role of the antivirus engine on a firewall?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Route packets
\item Scan files and traffic for malware
\item Assign IP addresses
\item Authenticate users
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} The antivirus engine inspects files and traffic to detect and block malware.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 13} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
An IPS differs from an IDS mainly because an IPS can:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Block traffic
\item Only log events
\item Operate out-of-band only
\item Never generate alerts
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} IPS can take active blocking actions, while IDS primarily detects.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 14} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Signature-based detection cannot easily detect:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Known attacks
\item Zero-day attacks
\item Old malware
\item Common exploits
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Zero-day attacks have no known signatures.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 15} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Sandboxing helps detect malware by:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Observing behavior in an isolated environment
\item Matching signatures
\item Comparing file sizes
\item Checking file names
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Sandboxing executes suspicious files safely to observe malicious behavior.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 16} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Antivirus engines require regular updates to:

\begin{qoptions}
\item Recognize new malware variants
\item Improve routing
\item Increase bandwidth
\item Disable firewalls
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Signature and detection databases must be updated to detect emerging threats.
\end{explainbox}
\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 17} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}

\begin{qoptions}
\item IDS
\item False negative
\item Reconnaissance
\item Exploitation

\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} IDS refers to the concept where detects intrusions and generates alerts.
\end{explainbox}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 18} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}

\begin{qoptions}
\item IPS
\item Anomaly detection
\item Exploitation
\item IDS

\textbf{Concept:} IPS refers to the concept where detects and blocks intrusions inline.

Which concept is described as: matches known attack patterns?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Signature detection
\item Heuristic analysis

\begin{explainbox}{A}
\textbf{Concept:} Signature detection refers to the concept where matches known attack patterns.

\item IPS

\item Antivirus signatures

\begin{explainbox}{B}
\textbf{Concept:} Anomaly detection refers to the concept where flags deviations from normal behavior.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 21} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: incorrectly blocks legitimate traffic?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Heuristic analysis

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 22} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: misses an actual attack?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Signature detection
\item False negative

\item False positive

\textbf{Concept:} False negative refers to the concept where misses an actual attack.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 23} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: observes file behavior in an isolated environment?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Antivirus signatures
\item HIDS
\item Sandboxing
\item Whitelisting
\end{qoptions}
\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Sandboxing refers to the concept where observes file behavior in an isolated environment.

\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 24} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: detects malware by examining behavior?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Sandboxing
\item HIDS
\item Heuristic analysis

\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Heuristic analysis refers to the concept where detects malware by examining behavior.

\end{qcard}

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 25} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: allows only approved applications?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Whitelisting
\item Reconnaissance
\item Signature detection

\end{qoptions}

\textbf{Concept:} Whitelisting refers to the concept where allows only approved applications.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 26} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: must be updated regularly?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Sandboxing
\item IDS
\item Antivirus signatures

\begin{explainbox}{C}
\textbf{Concept:} Antivirus signatures refers to the concept where must be updated regularly.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 27} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: monitors network traffic for intrusions?

\begin{qoptions}
\item Antivirus signatures

\item IPS
\item Exploitation

\textbf{Concept:} NIDS refers to the concept where monitors network traffic for intrusions.

\begin{qcard}{\color{white}\textbf{Question 28} \hfill \small Single Choice \textbar{} Topic: Intrusion Prevention and Antivirus}{singlecol}
Which concept is described as: monitors host activity for intrusions?

\begin{qoptions}
\item IDS
\item False positive
\item Heuristic analysis
\item HIDS

\begin{explainbox}{D}
\textbf{Concept:} HIDS refers to the concept where monitors host activity for intrusions.
